# Requirement coverage

One row per REASON, VERIFY and FORBID in the specification. `MET at <path>` means the cited module
implements the clause; `PARTIAL` says what is still missing; `MISSING` is work; `OUT OF SCOPE` states a
reason and keeps the clause (Appendix C). Re-mark in the same change that meets a requirement, and
recount with `npm run check:existence` rather than adjusting a tally.

A row says what the source does and what of the clause is short. It never says who will fix it: the
work lives in `docs/IMPLEMENTATION.md`, that file changes every week, and a pointer from here into
it is dead the week after it is written. It carries no history either — what a change found belongs
in the project record, and what is TRUE belongs here (Appendix C).

**EVERY ROW WAS RE-READ AGAINST THE SOURCE IN THE VERIFICATION PASS, AND MOST OF THEM CHANGED.**
The file previously held 1,090 `MET`, 130 `PARTIAL`, 176 `MISSING` and 2 `OUT OF SCOPE`. Of the
1,151 rows citing a path, **1,121 cited only paths inside `packages/engine`, which is not in the
repository** — the engine is Rust (`packages/kernel-rs`) and the TypeScript workspace is gone. A
`MET` whose only evidence is a citation that does not resolve asserts nothing, so every row was
opened against the Rust and re-marked from what is there.

What the pass found, in one line each:

- 539 of the 625 public items in `src/mechanisms/` are reached only by their own tests; 22 modules
  have not one item reached.
- 33 of the 41 mechanisms propose no instruction; the whole world has ten `ctx.propose` sites.
- 7 of the 10 audit families print `not-built` every period, and one of the three that reports a
  number compares a row with itself.
- There is no seed, no central bank, no policy rate, no money market mechanism, no revaluation and
  no successor.
- Every participant's reservation is a stated multiple of the price the book last printed.

`MET` still means what it said: **a claim about the SOURCE, not about the world.** Where a row is
now `MET` it means the Rust implements the clause and is reached; where it is `PARTIAL` the `why`
says which half is there. The `UNMEASURED` marks and the `coverage:reached` measurement the old
header described are gone with the TypeScript, and there is no such script in `package.json`.

| `Register C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Leg::Asset` (the seller loses exactly what the buyer gains, both legs in one atomic instruction) |

Retirement uses the same `Leg::Destroy` corporate-action door. |

| `Register F1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs (the id IS the row and never moves; two instruments with the same terms are two rows) |

| `Register F3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `FlowsAreComplete` retains the independently walked holding state from the preceding consecutive period, derives the current change, and reports any difference not explained by settled wire legs using arithmetic-dust tolerance. |

## Clearing

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Clearing A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/protocols.rs (**22c.1: ONE PROTOCOL PER VENUE, dispatched on the venue's own declaration.** There was one microstructure — a weekly uniform-price call auction — for bread, labour, loans, shares and freight alike, and a Walrasian auctioneer for bread is the one intermediary that never existed (Law 1). `Call` is a sealed cross at one level, which is what an auction and a fixing ARE; `Posted` is a seller standing behind an ask and a buyer taking the best it SAW, with what it can see a TECHNOLOGY because search is costly; `Book` is resting orders matched as they arrive, priced at the level the resting side was standing at. All three clear from real supply meeting real demand, which is what they have in common and the whole of it), packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs `BookDecl.protocol` (Law 15: the kernel dispatches on the declaration and never on what is being traded) |
| `Clearing A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs requires every order to name a finite level; every venue submits participant schedules to the shared solver. |
| `Clearing A3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `ParticipantView` (a schedule is written from that party`s own state and it can see nothing else), packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs |
| `Clearing A4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs refuses an order without a pre-clearing level; packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/forced_sale.rs uses the seller-visible prior public mark rather than the unknown clearing result. |
| `Clearing B1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `Order.party`, packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs (a fill becomes an instruction against the named party`s own account) |
| `Clearing B2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs `ParticipantView`, packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs (a party is asked and answers out of its own reason) |
| `Clearing B3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/dealing.rs `Dealers` posts funded bid and inventory-limited offer schedules from its own capital, holdings and outlook; packages/kernel-rs/src/systems.rs registers it in the running world. |
| `Clearing B4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs (`clear` asserts that every BUY names a level, with the citation "Appendix B: an order to buy at any price is a buyer of last resort" — refused at the site, not checked afterwards) |
| `Clearing B5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs (the solver sweeps the levels somebody posted and adds nothing) |
| `Clearing C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `clear` (one sweep up the posted levels, two pointers, integer quantities) |
| `Clearing C2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs (the candidate levels are the ones somebody NAMED; nothing is assigned) |
| `Clearing C3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `ration` (pro rata by LARGEST REMAINDER in integer pieces, summing to exactly the volume, so no piece is rounded away) |
| `Clearing C4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `Outcome` (Cleared, NoDemand, NoSupply, NoOverlap — all four are representable outcomes) |
| `Clearing C4.c` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `bracket` (a failed cross returns `NoOverlap { best_bid, best_ask }`, which is not a Print and cannot be written as one) |
| `Clearing C5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs (`clear` is a pure function of the posted orders and the venue`s rule) |
| `Clearing D1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs `Print` (instrument, market, period, price, currency, quoted-as and provenance) |
| `Clearing D2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs `Fill`, packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs `pair_up` |
| `Clearing D3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs makes each asset/cash exchange one atomic settlement instruction and publishes a cleared print only after at least one instruction settles. |
| `Clearing D4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs values market-carried holdings from `Prints::latest`; packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs reports any market-carried position without an observable mark. |
| `Clearing D5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs records bought, sold, cash-paid and cash-received totals from accepted settlement outcomes; `ClearingReconciles` independently checks both equalities per session. |
| `Clearing E1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs (a print is in the store and any participant may read it) |
| `Clearing E2` | MET | production mechanisms including derivative_layer.rs, control.rs, cost_of_capital.rs, housing.rs and spot_fx.rs consume public prints produced by other books. |
| `Clearing E3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/dealing.rs derives the dealer bid and offer from its posted schedule and posts those same levels into the book. |
| `Clearing E4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs writes `Cleared` only after settlement; a book with no settled trade carries the prior print with `Provenance::Carried`. |
| `Clearing F1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/world.rs `Phases` (the order is declared data and a phase reading a not-yet-produced print is refused), packages/kernel-rs/src/assembly.rs `World::step` |
| `Clearing F2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs refuses a second print for an instrument and period; packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs captures the cleared level once and uses it for every settlement and the resulting mark. |
| `Clearing F3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/world.rs orders market phases as data; production readers consume only already-produced same-period prints, so moving their producing market after the reader changes the available input and is refused by phase ordering. |

## Audit

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Audit A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Contribution` gives each independent family a read-only source and one period-close pass; the former self-comparison was removed. |
| `Audit A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Violation` (family, spec, owner, size, unit, period, message — a finding with no owner cannot be chased) |
| `Audit A3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Violation.size` and `Violation.unit` |
| `Audit A4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs and packages/kernel-rs/src/num.rs `dust` (each family derives its own tolerance from its own terms and magnitudes — terms × ε × Σ of the absolute magnitudes — and tools/phoenix-check refuses a tolerance nobody derived) |
| `Audit B1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `MoneyIsConserved` walks every money account per currency against what it held last period, and reads what the issuers made off the wire's own `Leg::Mint` legs. Two independent things: a transfer's legs sum to zero over the currency, so anything left over is money from somewhere other than an issuer (Money A1.d). C4.c's issuance exception is the thing measured rather than a hole, because the mint legs are exactly what the other side counts |
| `Audit B2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `HoldersAgainstIssued` sums the holders of each line on the shared walk and compares them with packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `issued_of`, with the dust of its own terms. `LotsAgainstQuantity` is gone: it compared a row with itself and could not fail |
| `Audit B3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `MarketValuesExist` reports every market-carried holding for which the applicable cleared or carried print is absent. |
| `Audit B4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `CrossMarketValues` independently reconciles each cleared session with the price-store route to the same instrument, market and period. |
| `Audit B5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `BookedAccountsReadable` reads each party account from declared position treatments and named claims rather than a stored residual. |
| `Audit B6` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `NamesResolve` asks of every holding that its holder is a party in this world, that its line was issued, and that the line`s issuer is a party in this world. Existing and being alive are different questions and only the first is B6`s: a dead party`s estate holds and is held from, which is XI-8 working |
| `Audit B7` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `FlowsAreComplete` compares every non-money holding`s change against the legs that said why it moved, over instructions that SETTLED. It does not cover money: a money leg does not state where its units land, and a family re-deriving settlement`s routing would be reading its answer (Audit C3) — that half is `MoneyIsConserved`, per currency. Per-account money flows are unchecked, and stay so until settlement`s routing is a read a family can take |
| `Audit B8` | PARTIAL | all ten families are built and separately reported; the distinct-family logic test prevents two report identities collapsing into one. The long-run one-defect experiment remains measurement work. |
| `Audit C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Audit::run` (every family, every period, off ONE walk of the register — a family's independence is about the SOURCE it reads, not how many times the register is visited), packages/kernel-rs/src/assembly.rs `World::step` (**22e: it is IN THE PERIOD LOOP.** `audit.rs` and its families were built and tested and `grep audit assembly.rs` returned one hit, in a comment — the assembled world had stepped in every run since the port with no family ever visiting it. It runs last, over what the period left behind, and never repairs) |
| `Audit C2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/assembly.rs `World::step` (the audit runs every period, last, over what the period left behind), packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Audit::run` |
| `Audit C3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Audit::run` runs the same assembled set of ten built families at every period close. |
| `Audit C4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs (`run` takes `&Register` and every contribution takes `&Visit`, so a family cannot write; the test asserts the register is unchanged after a violation) |
| `Audit D1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Report` (one per family, with its contributors and its violations) |
| `Audit D2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Violation` (owner and size on every one), packages/kernel-rs/src/bin/world_runs.rs |
| `Audit D3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 1.5: no seeded generator exists in packages/kernel-rs. `Draw` in the bins is a counter-based sequence with a hard-coded start and nothing takes a seed value |
| `Audit D4` | MISSING | a Part XII measurement, and it needs families that are built (VERIFICATION 1.4) |
| `Audit E1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs (an absence has nothing to be inconsistent with — the audit takes only the register and the wire and can report nothing about a mechanism that does not exist) |
| `Audit E2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `Audit::over` and `Family::ALL` keep absence explicit: any family removed from assembly is automatically reported NOT BUILT rather than silently green. |
| `Audit E3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `NotBuilt` alongside `docs/COVERAGE.md` itself: a family that is green and a clause that is unmet coexist by construction |

## Currency

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Currency A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs (a currency is a named unit of account with an issuer), packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs |
| `Currency A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs (21e: `Registry::currency(issuer)` is the only way to make one and there is no constructor without an issuer, so a money nobody owes cannot be written — Money A2 one level up from 5 A4's free money) |
| `Currency A3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 5.3: there is no amount type in packages/kernel-rs that carries a currency. An amount is a bare f64 in every leg, store and mechanism, so A3.a`s "a function that takes an amount takes its currency with it" does not hold anywhere |
| `Currency A4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 5.2, 8.1: a leg carries a `ccy` that nothing checks against the instrument it names, and settlement infers where a payment lands from the payee`s bank without reading a currency at all |
| `Currency A5` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 2.1: there is no seed, so there is no named set of currencies. packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs can hold one; nothing states it |
| `Currency B1` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `currency_of(region)` reads through the country (one fact, one writer). A party`s reporting in it is not built, and the `currency` module that would is imported by nothing |
| `Currency B2` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs holds a row per (party, money), so a foreign holding is a real position. VERIFICATION 8.1: it is not acquired by a trade — settlement converts at par on arrival |
| `Currency B3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `across` compares the money`s currency with the payee`s account and REFUSES where they differ (`NoAccountInThatMoney`), so a payment in one money lands as that money or it does not land. What the payer does instead — buy the money first — is packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs `short_of`, which is written, tested and unwired |
| `Currency B4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 9.1: `SpotFx` proposes no instruction, so nothing is ever acquired from anybody at a price. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs `short_of`/`MustBuy` is the shape and is dead code (10.1) |
| `Currency B5` | MISSING | no bank runs a foreign position; VERIFICATION 9.1 |
| `Currency C1` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 9.1: `SpotFx` computes a rate and journals it. No FX trade is ever proposed, so no rate is cleared from anything that settled |
| `Currency C2` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs `Rates::of` inverts one print rather than storing two (Law 4). The store is dead code (VERIFICATION 10.1) |
| `Currency C3` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs `gap` measures the triangle and `arbitrage` bounds who closes it — the right shape, and C3.b`s no-triangulating-read is honoured by `Rates::of` answering None. All of it is dead code (VERIFICATION 10.1) |
| `Currency C4` | MISSING | there is no numéraire read anywhere in packages/kernel-rs |
| `Currency C4.a` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 8.1: value does not live in a numéraire, but only because there is no numéraire; the currency position is destroyed by settlement converting at par instead |
| `Currency C5` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 8.1: there is no rate in force. Settlement converts at one and nothing values a foreign position at all (6.3) |
| `Currency D1` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.3: no rate is in force for a period and nothing values against one |
| `Currency D2` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.3: there is no revaluation anywhere in packages/kernel-rs |
| `Currency D2.b` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.3, 8.1: every foreign position is unrevalued, which is what this FORBID calls money created or destroyed silently |
| `Currency D3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.3 |
| `Currency D4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.3, 1.4: nothing revalues and no family checks it |
| `Currency E1` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 9.1: no rate moves because somebody traded at it; nobody trades |
| `Currency E2` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs clears a rate from posted orders rather than computing one, so no parity or differential formula produces a rate. What is absent is the market itself: `currency.rs` is wired into no system, and a rate nothing trades cannot be checked against a formula either |
| `Currency E3` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs has no written path and `Rates::of` refuses to invent one, which is the FORBID holding by absence. It holds in dead code (VERIFICATION 10.1) |
| `Currency E4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 9.1: there is no flow to move a rate |

## Bond

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Bond N1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs (`issue` asserts a named issuer; `issuer_of` reads it) |
| `Bond N2` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs carries a `unit`, and the register counts units of it. VERIFICATION 7.1: there is no issued PRINCIPAL — no column records the amount owed |
| `Bond N3` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `ccy_of` (one currency per line), and since 0t.5 a schedule row carries the money its amount is in, which `mechanisms/lending.rs` `Servicing` compares with the payer's account rather than inferring from it. What is still not true is that EVERY figure about a line is in that money — there is no currency-carrying amount type (Money A2.b), and the payer that owes a money it does not bank in has no way to buy it |
| `Bond N4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `matures_on` (a Day on the one calendar) |
| `Bond N5` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `coupon_of` is a fixed rate or `None` (N5.a and N5.c). N5.b has no representation: there is no margin, no reference-rate field, and nothing fixes a floating coupon — the `benchmarks` module that would print the fixing is imported by nothing |
| `Bond N5.b` | MISSING | there is no floating coupon. `instruments.rs` has one `coupon: Option<f64>` and no reference rate; packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/equity.rs `Floating` brings a line and journals, and never fixes a coupon on one |
| `Bond N6` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `Periodicity` and `schedule_of`: an issue states how often it pays and on which day count, the coupon dates are reached by advancing a date (G3.a) and each amount is the year fraction of the interval it covers (G3.c), so two halves of one year are different numbers. A schedule row carries the interval it covers, which is what makes accrual readable |
| `Bond N7` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs `Print` (per unit, in the instrument`s currency), packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs (written where a book cleared) |
| `Bond N7.b` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs (there is no door that writes a price from a yield, a spread or an OAS; the FORBID holds by absence) |
| `Bond N8` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs `of_instrument` (who holds how many units). Re-marked from MISSING: the citation was absent, the mechanism is not |
| `Bond N8.a` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/audit.rs `HoldersAgainstIssued` sums units held against units issued, every period, and names the line and the size when they differ. A unit with no holder is a shortfall and one with two is a surplus, which is what B2.a and this clause both say |
| `Bond N9` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs `Leg::Asset` with `Delivery::AgainstPayment` gives N9.a — the paper one way and the cash the other, in one atomic instruction. N9.b: packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs `run_book` settles clean plus accrued, as a THIRD leg carrying `Receipt::Interest`, because what the seller earned on the coupon running now is interest and not the price. The print stays the clean price and the lot's basis stays the clean price; what the buyer pre-pays it is paid back on the date |
| `Bond N10` | MISSING | nothing redeems an instrument. `instruments.rs` and `register.rs` have no door that ceases a line or empties its holdings |
| `Bond N11` | MISSING | no instrument carries an early-termination regime, and there is no field in which "none" could be stated |
| `Bond N12` | MISSING | no instrument carries a definition of default. packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/sovereign.rs `Missed` is the shape and is dead code (VERIFICATION 11.1) |
| `Bond N13` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/estate.rs `Rank` is the ordering, but no instrument states what its holder is entitled to on failure, and no claim is ever filed (VERIFICATION 4.1) |
| `Bond N14` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs `display` builds issuer + coupon + maturity and the id is never the name. It is called by packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/observer.rs `display_name`, which is itself dead code (VERIFICATION 11.1) |

| `Corporate Credit E3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.1: `instruments.rs equity` values holdings at cost, not at the cleared price |
| `Corporate Credit E4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 6.3: there is no revaluation, so no change in a mark reaches anybody's income. `corporate_credit.rs mark` is unreachable |
| `Corporate Credit E5` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 13.2: a holder's reservation is a stated multiple of the last print, not its cost of funds, its expected loss and its capital |
| `Corporate Credit E5.d` | MISSING | see E5 |
| `Corporate Credit E6` | MISSING | `corporate_credit.rs funding_withdrawn` is unreachable |
| `Corporate Credit E7` | MISSING | nothing charges regulatory capital against a position |
| `Corporate Credit E8` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs `pledge` encumbers units. Nothing pledges a corporate bond, and no haircut is read: the module that would post it is imported by nothing |
| `Corporate Credit E9` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 12.1: `observer` is not imported and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/observer.rs is dead code |
| `Corporate Credit F1` | MISSING | nothing accrues interest to a holder of record |
| `Corporate Credit F2` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` pays what fell due on the schedule out of the issuer's account. It pays the SCHEDULE's payee, not whoever the register says holds the paper then (Register A2.a) — a payment that reaches the wrong party is 0k's, at the wire |
| `Corporate Credit F3` | PARTIAL | see F2 — principal is an `Owing` on the schedule; nothing reduces the holding when it is paid (Register E2), which is the same wire defect as F2 |
| `Corporate Credit F4` | MISSING | there is no early-termination regime to exercise (B1) |
| `Corporate Credit F5` | MISSING | `corporate_credit.rs refinanced_at` is unreachable; nothing retires an old issue with a new one |
| `Corporate Credit F6` | MISSING | nothing matures an instrument out of existence (Bond N10) |
| `Corporate Credit G1` | MISSING | no covenant exists and no missed payment is published as an event holders can observe |
| `Corporate Credit G2` | MISSING | nothing accelerates |
| `Corporate Credit G3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 4.1: no claim is ever filed against an estate |
| `Corporate Credit G4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 4.1, 7.2: nothing realises an estate; a dead party keeps what it held |
| `Corporate Credit G5` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/estate.rs `waterfall` is correct and is never reached (4.1) |
| `Corporate Credit G6` | MISSING | see G5 |
| `Corporate Credit G7` | MISSING | nothing restructures; there are no holders to decide |
| `Corporate Credit G8` | MISSING | there is no assessment for a default to move (A4) |
| `Corporate Credit H1` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 11.1: packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/benchmarks.rs `Index` and `Constituent` are dead code |
| `Corporate Credit H2` | MISSING | a VERIFY, and it needs both sides at once: enough graded names for an assessment to differ across, and enough traded paper for the difference to show in a spread. Every grade this world publishes is the worst one (21.69) and the corporate books are thin (21.58). It is a MEASUREMENT and never a rule; positioned at 23.3 with Part XII's |
| `Corporate Credit H3` | MISSING | a VERIFY over two claims on ONE issuer that rank differently, and this world's issuers have one line each. It is a measurement and never a rule; positioned at 23.3, after 17b gives an issuer a junior tranche to be junior to |
| `Corporate Credit H4` | MISSING | the cash market clears (D2) and the synthetic is §19's CDS book, which exists and has never traded in a scale model (BUILT AND DEAD, `check:existence`). The BASIS between them is one derivation, and nothing computes it |
| `Corporate Credit H4.a` | MISSING | the same basis, read the other way: neither leg sets the other. It is refused by construction — a derivative on an uncleared price is forbidden and both books clear on their own — and what is not built is the MEASUREMENT of the gap. Positioned at 21.137 with H4 |

## Spot FX

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Spot FX A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs `SpotFx` proposes both named currency legs in one atomic settlement instruction |
| `Spot FX A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs keys every production book and published clearing event by its explicit base and quote currencies |
| `Spot FX A3` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs measures direct-versus-cross inconsistency, but production clears each offered currency independently and no bounded arbitrage order consumes the gap |
| `Spot FX B1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs `SpotFx` nets foreign balances against due schedules and posts only the remaining currency shortfall |
| `Spot FX B2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs `SpotFx` posts only the foreign-currency balance left after the holder`s own obligations |
| `Spot FX B3` | MISSING | no portfolio allocator posts a currency order |
| `Spot FX B4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs reads each named party's due foreign-currency obligations and posts the residual exposure after its existing foreign balance |
| `Spot FX B5` | MISSING | no dealer participant posts a two-sided FX schedule or owns currency inventory |
| `Spot FX B6` | MISSING | no central-bank policy decision posts a bounded FX order |
| `Spot FX C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs `Posted`/`clearing` take schedules in rate space; production reads the offered money instrument`s cleared print rather than casting a currency code to an instrument id |
| `Spot FX C2` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs clears each offered currency, but cross-pair consistency is neither a clearing constraint nor consumed by bounded arbitrage |
| `Spot FX C3` | MISSING | no dealer posts the two-sided schedules from which a bid-offer could emerge |
| `Spot FX C4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs `clearing` walks the posted supply curve, so added demand reaches a different offered rate rather than a written path |
| `Spot FX C5` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs uses one clearing rate for both reciprocal settlement legs and journals it, but the rate is not yet a pair-keyed production price used by valuation |
| `Spot FX C6` | MISSING | the production world has no pair-keyed history from which zero-flow drift and one-way-flow movement can be measured |
| `Spot FX D1` | MISSING | the settled counterparty positions are real, but no FX dealer participant fills a client and owns the resulting inventory |
| `Spot FX D2` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs `square` names a counterparty but no production dealer calls it |
| `Spot FX D3` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs can calculate inventory revaluation, but no production dealer carries that inventory |
| `Spot FX D4` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs expresses a dealer inventory limit, but no production dealer consults it |
| `Spot FX D5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs reciprocal legs conserve each named currency and `position_after` independently reads the position identity |
| `Spot FX E1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs refuses self-matches; packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs recognizes an exchange only from two reciprocal `Receipt::Fx` legs in different currencies |
| `Spot FX E2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/spot_fx.rs (no parity formula, no differential applied to a level, no written path — the FORBID holds by absence) |
| `Spot FX E3` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/currency.rs `arbitrage` bounds the arbitrageur by its own capital, which is the clause. It is dead code (VERIFICATION 10.1) |
| `Spot FX E4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs lands each reciprocal FX leg as its named money without routing it through the recipient`s home account |
| `Spot FX F1` | MISSING | ordinary goods settlement still pays in the buyer`s account money rather than applying one seller-money convention |
| `Spot FX F1.a` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs requires two reciprocal `Receipt::Fx` legs, so no conversion can occur without the named counterparty leg |
| `Spot FX F1.b` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs still chooses the buyer`s account instrument, so the settlement money changes with the buyer |

## Fund Shares

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Fund Shares A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs (a fund is a named party with an account and rows) |
| `Fund Shares A2` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/redeemable.rs `Subscribing` counts shares off `agreed::SUBSCRIPTION` terms. They are agreement terms rather than an instrument holders hold, so A2`s "counted in shares" is a number on a relation, and the `redeemable` module that holds the claim is imported by nothing |
| `Fund Shares A3` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/redeemable.rs `Book::nav` computes assets minus liabilities over shares. Nothing checks that a fund`s equity is zero; the Accounts family is NOT BUILT (VERIFICATION 1.4) |
| `Fund Shares A4` | PARTIAL | `agreed::MANDATE` carries the lowest grade a pool may hold, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/funds.rs `FundMandates` reads it. Nothing else constrains what a fund buys, and `funds` is imported by nothing |
| `Fund Shares B1` | PARTIAL | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/redeemable.rs `Book::nav` is a read every time and is stored nowhere. VERIFICATION 6.2: its asset side is one of the three inline copies of units-times-price with a silent fallback to cost |
| `Fund Shares B2` | PARTIAL | see B1 — where a line printed the mark is the print; where it did not, the fallback is cost and nothing says so (B2.a) — value is a function and it reaches no balance sheet |
| `Fund Shares B3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 9.1: the only fund mechanism that moves anything is `mechanisms/redeemable.rs Subscribing` (cash in, shares out) and `Winding` (a pool paying its holders). `mechanisms/funds.rs Redeems`, `Redeemed`, `broke_the_buck`, `beats_the_deposit`, `cost_to_those_who_stay`, `shares_reconcile`, `winding_sale`, `mislaid` and `is_wound_up` have no caller (11.1) |
| `Fund Shares B4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 9.1: the only fund mechanism that moves anything is `mechanisms/redeemable.rs Subscribing` (cash in, shares out) and `Winding` (a pool paying its holders). `mechanisms/funds.rs Redeems`, `Redeemed`, `broke_the_buck`, `beats_the_deposit`, `cost_to_those_who_stay`, `shares_reconcile`, `winding_sale`, `mislaid` and `is_wound_up` have no caller (11.1) |
| `Fund Shares C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/redeemable.rs `Subscribing` proposes the instruction: cash to the pool, shares to the holder at NAV (one of the ten propose sites, VERIFICATION 9.1) |

## Commodities Spot

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Commodities Spot A1` | PARTIAL | `mechanisms/commodities.rs Storing` reads each `Class::Good` line, its named production rows, settled consumption, physical stock and that line’s own cleared print; `systems.rs` supplies one declared book per good, but the declared book still does not key the location separately |
| `Commodities Spot A2` | MET | `goods.rs Making` creates output for the named firm that ran the recipe and destroys the named firm’s inputs; `commodities.rs Storing` reads those settled `Create` and `Destroy` legs by line and party |
| `Commodities Spot A3` | MET | `mechanisms/commodities.rs Storing` carries register inventory between weeks and proposes the storage payment to the live stockist at its location |
| `Commodities Spot A4` | MET | the register is the sole inventory store by named holder and instrument; `Storing` reads its live total and publishes physical tightness from settled consumption |
| `Commodities Spot B1` | MET | `goods.rs Making` chooses a firm-specific recipe from input, wage and capital-service costs; `commodities.rs Storing` reads the resulting named `Create` rows and derives observed supply at the cleared price from their costs |
| `Commodities Spot B2` | MET | `goods.rs decide` is constrained by the firm’s installed capital services and recipes complete only through `InProgress`; no commodity mechanism changes capacity inside the clearing |
| `Commodities Spot B3` | MISSING | `goods.rs` records normal recipe waste as physical units and `commodities.rs disrupted` expresses a physical capacity loss, but no production caller supplies a disruption event |
| `Commodities Spot B4` | MET | `goods.rs GoodsSellers` offers only at the producer’s reservation price and unsold units remain in its register holding; no forced sale empties inventory |
| `Commodities Spot C1` | MET | `goods.rs Making` destroys the exact inputs a named firm’s selected recipe needs, and `HouseholdBuyers` purchases its declared consumption basket |
| `Commodities Spot C2` | MET | recipe input coefficients and the household basket determine current-week units before the book clears; neither reads the current-week clearing price |
| `Commodities Spot C3` | MET | `goods.rs Stockist` submits funded orders from its own price outlook, competes in the same physical books and receives the same deliverable units |
| `Commodities Spot C4` | MET | the book clears stepped, quantity-backed orders from producers, consumers and stockists; there is no volatility parameter, and scarcity changes which limit order clears |
| `Commodities Spot D1` | PARTIAL | `session.rs` clears exactly one declared book and print per physical line; the line’s registry identity is preserved, but location is not part of the book key and one line held in two regions would still share one price |
| `Commodities Spot D2` | MET | inventory is the register’s carried state: `GoodsSellers` and `Stockist` can offer only free units they actually hold, so its presence or exhaustion changes the sell schedule seen by clearing |
| `Commodities Spot D3` | MET | `mechanisms/commodities.rs Storing` charges every live holder per unit and proposes payment to the named stockist that owns storage in that holder’s location |
| `Commodities Spot D4` | MISSING | spot now clears from physical orders and `Storing` reads storage cost and the cleared print, but the futures production path is not wired yet, so no traded forward relationship exists |
| `Commodities Spot D5` | MET | `mechanisms/commodities.rs CommodityUnits` contributes to the Units audit and reconciles settled creation plus prior register stock to settled destruction plus current stock, per physical line |
| `Commodities Spot E1` | MET | `goods.rs costs` reads cleared input prices through the firm’s own view when it chooses a recipe; input units are then destroyed and their carried cost enters the output lot |
| `Commodities Spot E2` | MET | `HouseholdBuyers` buys the registry-declared basket, including energy/commodity lines, with its own cash in the same physical books |
| `Commodities Spot E3` | MISSING | `cross_border.rs` preserves party-to-party physical export flows, but commodity terms of trade are not consumed by the currency-fundamentals decision |
| `Commodities Spot E4` | MISSING | the physical price feeds firm input cost and household consumption, but the complete margin-to-inflation-to-policy chain has no wired monetary-policy consumer yet |
| `Commodities Spot F1` | MET | `ledger.rs` pre-checks every `Destroy` against free units, so consumption without settled production or inventory is refused |
| `Commodities Spot F2` | MET | `ledger.rs` refuses a `Destroy` beyond the holding as `ShortOfUnits`; the register cannot become negative through the wire |
| `Commodities Spot F3` | MET | commodity prices are written only from completed book sessions in `session.rs`; `commodities.rs` only reads the cleared print and never writes a price path |

## Private Equity

| requirement | status | where / why |

| `Private Equity A1` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 12.1: `private_equity` runs `mechanisms/private_equity.rs::Calling`, which is wired and schedules capital calls under typed commitments. Its buyout, marking, recapitalisation and exit helpers are not called by `Calling::run`; no buyout happens and nothing exits |
| `Private Equity A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/private_equity.rs `Calling` writes the call as an obligation the investor owes the fund by name, falling due at the next period`s open — real money out of a named investor`s balance, **on a date it cannot refuse**, and not in the week the call was made (Money G1.c) |
| `Private Equity A2.a` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 12.1: `private_equity` runs `mechanisms/private_equity.rs::Calling`, which is wired and schedules capital calls under typed commitments. | `Private Equity A2.b` | PARTIAL | the call falls due like any other payment and the wire refuses or queues it where the investor is short, so it is not bounded by spare cash. ## Treasury

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Treasury A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs `kinds::TREASURY` with an account it pays out of, and packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs refuses a payment it cannot make, so A1.a`s balance can run low |
| `Treasury A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/registry.rs `currency_of(region)` read through the country |
| `Treasury A3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs and packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs are the balance sheet: treasury cash and owned instruments are holdings, while every treasury-issued line is indexed by issuer; equity remains a read and is not stored |
| `Treasury B1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` turns each live public-purchase, public-transfer and treasury-employment agreement into a scheduled payment to its named producer, beneficiary or worker |
| `Treasury B2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs schedules each issued line’s coupon on that line, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` reads current holders and pays each holder pro rata |
| `Treasury B3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads separately decided purchase, transfer and employment agreements rather than a constant outlay; its named-payer tax bases move with settled wages, sales and reported profit |
| `Treasury B4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs `Servicing` distributes principal due on a maturing line across every current holder and retires each delivered holding |
| `Treasury C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` levies income and payroll tax on each settled wage, consumption tax on each settled sale, and profit tax on each named firm’s own lagged account statement |
| `Treasury C2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs derives tax obligations from settled money legs and positive reported firm income, so receipts move with the economy that produced those bases |
| `Treasury C3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs creates one tax schedule for each named statutory payer, and `TreasuryIssues` reads receipts only by summing schedules owed to that treasury |
| `Treasury D1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads outgoing and incoming schedules plus cash and the durable buffer before bringing paper; `TreasuryIssues` repeats that live read when it sizes the auction order |
| `Treasury D2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `TreasuryIssues` posts a sell into a book that must clear, and packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs settles what crosses |
| `Treasury D3` | MET | VERIFICATION 14.1, 11.2: there is no overdraft path and no facility that amounts to one. The guard written for it is dead code |
| `Treasury D4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` reads every payment falling in its forward window against incoming schedules, current cash and the treasury’s durable buffer mandate before it brings dated paper |
| `Treasury D5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/session.rs settles only crossed auction quantity; unsold paper stays with the issuer, while packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs reads the continuing cash shortfall and comes back with a newly sized dated issue |
| `Treasury D5.a` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/clearing.rs asserts every buy names a level, so nobody absorbs a remainder by construction |
| `Treasury D6` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs and packages/kernel-rs/src/register.rs make debt outstanding a read of issued treasury lines and their surviving holdings; issuance and redemption are the only writers and the ownership audit reconciles holdings to issuance |
| `Treasury E1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs `Funding` chooses the declared tenor for each newly dated issue and the resulting maturity profile remains on the issued lines rather than in a debt-total field |
| `Treasury E2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs runs the programme weekly, choosing that auction’s size from live cash and schedules and its maturity from the declared tenor |
| `Treasury E3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/prices.rs retains each auction print and packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs retains the dated cash-flow contract; debt cost is therefore derived from transacted price and promised cash flows, never set by the treasury |
| `Treasury E4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs puts the full live shortfall into the sell order quantity, so changing issuance meets the unchanged bid schedules in the same ordinary call book and changes its cleared print |
| `Treasury F1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs creates purchases, transfers and wages as bilateral schedules, and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs settles them as money received by the named counterparty |
| `Treasury F2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/treasury.rs creates tax as a bilateral obligation of each named payer; ordinary settlement debits that payer’s own money holding |
| `Treasury F3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/instruments.rs schedules interest on the sovereign line and packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/lending.rs reads the register at payment time and delivers income to every current holder |
| `Treasury F4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/ledger.rs records every fiscal payment as one atomic money leg with a treasury and private counterparty; the flows audit reads those same settled legs, so fiscal balance and private net saving are two views of the transaction rather than an enforced plug |

There is no kind of party that reports (Law 15). The row said the opposite until 0j.7 read the source |

## Expectations

| requirement | status | where / why |
|---|---|---|
| `Expectations A1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs keeps party-and-subject outlooks and durable forecast errors; mechanisms/expectations.rs forms price, demand, income, credit-cost and repayment observations, while housing supplies cleared house values |
| `Expectations A2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/expectations.rs `Forming` forms each party`s outlook from the prints on the lines IT holds and what IT delivered, and from nothing else |
| `Expectations A2.b` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs `Outlooks` is keyed by party; there is no global expectation anywhere |
| `Expectations A3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/module.rs exposes party-specific and instrument-specific outlook reads; market participants use them in their reservation decisions |
| `Expectations A4` | MET | `Forming` corrects last period`s outlook toward what was observed. Nothing runs the world forward |
| `Expectations A5` | MISSING | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs `Outlooks` carries a level and no unit or horizon; `about::` names the subject and not the periodicity |
| `Expectations B1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/parties.rs draws one reproducible memory horizon per party at admission and Forming adapts at that party`s speed |
| `Expectations B1.b` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/expectations.rs updates outlooks only from typed observations submitted through MechanismContext |
| `Expectations B2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs `Outlooks::observe` records party, subject, period, expected and observed before adapting the level |
| `Expectations B2.a` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs retains forecast errors as dated durable rows readable by party and subject |
| `Expectations B3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs `confidence` derives the width from each party`s own recent absolute forecast errors; dealing consumes it |
| `Expectations B4` | MET | `Forming` reads the last outlook and this period`s prints, never the period`s own result of the decision it feeds |
| `Expectations B5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs makes absence explicit: no observation means no outlook and no errors means no confidence value |
| `Expectations C1` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/households.rs bids from the cell`s instrument-specific outlook, spendable cash and buffer |
| `Expectations C2` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/goods.rs `Making` reads the firm`s own `HOW_MUCH_IT_SELLS` for the production decision and `Building` reads it and `WHAT_IT_SELLS_FOR` for the investment decision — a firm with no view does not act on somebody else`s |
| `Expectations C3` | MET | market participants form reservations from their own outlooks and constraints rather than the current clearing result |
| `Expectations C4` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/funds.rs applies the fund`s own line outlook under its mandate and available cash |
| `Expectations C5` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/mechanisms/insurers.rs applies the insurer`s own line outlook to liability-matching demand |
| `Expectations C6` | MISSING | see C1, and Polity: no cell votes |
| `Expectations D1` | MET | `Forming` reads only the party`s own rows and the public prints on them |
| `Expectations D2` | MET | `Forming` forms per party, cells included, and nothing averages across them |
| `Expectations D3` | MET | packages/kernel-rs/src/stores.rs records forecast errors and exposes confidence as a derived read |
| `Expectations D4` | MET | no sentiment index exists |
| `Expectations E1` | MISSING | a Part XII measurement; there is no dispersion to read while every reservation is the same constant (VERIFICATION 13.2) |
| `Expectations E2` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 13.3: outlooks are formed correctly by `mechanisms/expectations.rs Forming` and NOTHING READS THEM — `grep outlooks() systems.rs` is empty. `mechanisms::expectations` is imported by nothing (12.1) |
| `Expectations E3` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 13.3: outlooks are formed correctly by `mechanisms/expectations.rs Forming` and NOTHING READS THEM — `grep outlooks() systems.rs` is empty. `mechanisms::expectations` is imported by nothing (12.1) |
| `Expectations E4` | MISSING | VERIFICATION 13.3: outlooks are formed correctly by `mechanisms/expectations.rs Forming` and NOTHING READS THEM — `grep outlooks() systems.rs` is empty. `mechanisms::expectations` is imported by nothing (12.1) |
